filing is a PDF whose facts are recovered by the shared \textbf{PDF extractor} (Chapter~\ref{ch:pdf}), of
which China is the flagship (and first CJK) consumer. Because disclosure is split across the \textbf{CSRC}
(listed companies), the \textbf{MOF}/\textbf{SASAC} channel (state-owned enterprises, largely internal) and
\textbf{AMAC} (funds), the chapter opens --- as India's did --- with a \emph{Repositories} survey
(\S\ref{sec:china-repos}): the central question is which door to build on. The answer is
\textbf{CNINFO} (\cncjk{巨潮资讯网}{}), the designated national disclosure portal,
whose keyless JSON API resolves an issuer and lists its annual-report PDFs. Financials are prepared under
\textbf{CAS/ASBE} (Chinese Accounting Standards) --- broadly
IFRS-converged but with a national taxonomy that is itself geo-blocked (\S\ref{sec:china-repos}), and, more
to the point, \emph{not} present in the PDFs at all: a PDF carries a Chinese line-item \emph{label}
(``Total assets''), not a namespaced concept. Classification is
therefore \textbf{by Chinese label}, through a dedicated \texttt{vocab\_cas.jl}. Identity is the
packed CNINFO code-plus-\texttt{orgId} (\texttt{EntityId(:cninfo, "000001,gssz0000001")}) --- the six-digit
exchange code together with CNINFO's internal organisation id, resolved from a bare code or company name the
way DART resolves a \texttt{stock\_code} to a \texttt{corp\_code}.

\emph{Status:} C0--C3 are \textbf{implemented}. The seam (C0 --- the \texttt{China} \texttt{FilingSystem},
its credential tag and keyless browser headers), CNINFO discovery (C1) and \texttt{fetch\_filing}~$\to$~PDF
\texttt{Filing} (C2) are done; \textbf{CAS facts (C3)} extract the consolidated balance sheet, income
statement and cash flow from the annual-report PDF through \textbf{Docling}, classified by the dedicated
\texttt{vocab\_cas.jl} (\S\ref{sec:china-taxonomy}). It is verified --- the balance sheet cross-foots and the
cash-flow identity holds --- on the \textbf{general-industry, combined (合并及公司) and bank}
financial-institution formats, across all three exchanges (\S\ref{sec:china-retrieve}). The validation
oracle (C4) and the insurer format are the remaining work.

\section{Repositories}\label{sec:china-repos}

There is no single ``Chinese EDGAR'' in the sense of one regulator's document store --- but there is a
single \emph{portal} that unifies the listed-company universe: \textbf{CNINFO}, operated by a Shenzhen
Securities Information Co.\ (an SZSE
subsidiary) under CSRC mandate, which carries the periodic reports of \emph{every} company listed on the
Shanghai, Shenzhen and Beijing exchanges. That makes the ``which door'' decision easier than India's: the
exchanges and the regulator all route public disclosure through CNINFO, so \texttt{EDGAR.jl} builds on it
directly (Table~\ref{tab:china-repos}). The distinguishing constraint is \emph{format}, not access ---
CNINFO is keyless and complete, but it serves \textbf{PDF}, not XBRL.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{Chinese financial-disclosure repositories and their accessibility.}
\label{tab:china-repos}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.16\textwidth}p{0.34\textwidth}p{0.22\textwidth}p{0.16\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Repository} & \textbf{What it is} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{CNINFO} & CSRC-designated national disclosure portal; periodic reports of all SSE/SZSE/BSE listed companies & \textbf{Keyless} JSON API; direct \texttt{.PDF} URLs on a static host & \textbf{primary channel} \\
\textbf{SSE} data service & Shanghai exchange's XBRL-\emph{derived} performance viewer; numbers normalised to base units & HTML/JSONP display layer; ``refer to the PDF for actual disclosed figures'' & display-only (not a parse target) \\
\textbf{SZSE} XBRL platform & Shenzhen exchange's 2009-era instance-document service & Legacy; no documented live keyless API & out of scope (legacy) \\
\textbf{CSRC} & The market regulator itself & Delegates public disclosure to CNINFO + the exchanges & not a fetch target \\
\textbf{MOF} taxonomy & The CAS General-Purpose national-GAAP vocabulary & Host \emph{geo-blocked} outside China & unreachable (and unneeded --- PDFs carry no concepts) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{CNINFO --- the primary channel (keyless)}
CNINFO behaves like the discovery-to-document seam of DART, but the document is a \textbf{PDF} and there is
\textbf{no API key} --- only a browser \texttt{User-Agent} and a CNINFO \texttt{Referer}. Unlike NSE it needs
no cookie bootstrap. The access flow is three requests: resolve the issuer to a code-plus-\texttt{orgId},
list its annual-report announcements, then download the PDF named in each record.

\begin{lstlisting}[style=term]
# UA is any realistic desktop-browser User-Agent; a CNINFO Referer is required throughout.

# 1. Resolve an issuer -- a 6-digit code or a company name -> {code, orgId}.
%  curl -A "$UA" -H "Referer: http://www.cninfo.com.cn/" \
     --data "keyWord=000001&maxNum=10" \
     http://www.cninfo.com.cn/new/information/topSearch/query
#  -> [{"code":"000001","orgId":"gssz0000001","zwjc":"..","category":"A股",..}]

# 2. List a company's annual reports (keyless JSON). stock = "code,orgId";
#    column = szse | sse | bj ; category_ndbg_szsh = the annual-report class.
%  curl -A "$UA" -H "Referer: http://www.cninfo.com.cn/" \
     --data "stock=000001,gssz0000001&column=szse&category=category_ndbg_szsh&tabName=fulltext&pageNum=1&pageSize=30&seDate=2024-01-01~2025-12-31" \
     http://www.cninfo.com.cn/new/hisAnnouncement/query

# 3. Download the PDF named in each record's `adjunctUrl` (prepend the static host).
%  curl -A "$UA" -H "Referer: http://www.cninfo.com.cn/" -o report.pdf \
     http://static.cninfo.com.cn/finalpage/2025-03-15/1222806505.PDF
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent Each discovery record looks like the following (fields abridged); the \texttt{adjunctUrl} (on the
\texttt{static.cninfo.com.cn} host) is the parse target, and \texttt{secCode}$+$\texttt{orgId} is the
identity key. Note the pairing of the full report with its \emph{summary} --- a separate, shorter PDF the
adapter must distinguish from the report proper.

\begin{lstlisting}[style=term]
{
  "secCode":           "000001",
  "secName":           "..",                      # issuer short name (Chinese)
  "orgId":             "gssz0000001",
  "announcementTitle": "2024 Annual Report",      # or ".. Annual Report Summary"
  "announcementTime":  1742054400000,             # epoch ms
  "adjunctType":       "PDF",
  "adjunctUrl":        "finalpage/2025-03-15/1222806505.PDF"
}
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent Two practical notes. First, the \texttt{topSearch} resolver is \textbf{live per query}, so ---
unlike DART's bulk \texttt{corpCode.xml} index --- China needs \emph{no} downloaded entity index; a bare
code or a name resolves to its \texttt{orgId} in one request. Second, a CNINFO annual-report query returns
\emph{both} the report and its summary; \texttt{EDGAR.jl} keeps the full report as the parse target and
treats the summary as secondary.

\subsection{SSE and SZSE --- the exchange data services}
Both exchanges expose company-financials views \emph{derived} from submitted filings, but neither is a clean
parse target. The Shanghai (SSE) data service renders a normalised balance-sheet/income/cash-flow view
behind a JSONP query layer, and states on its own pages that the figures are converted to base units and
that the \textbf{PDF} is authoritative for the disclosed numbers and units. The Shenzhen (SZSE) XBRL platform
is a 2009-era instance-document service with no documented live keyless API. Because CNINFO already carries
the authoritative PDFs for both exchanges' issuers, \texttt{EDGAR.jl} takes CNINFO and treats the exchange
data services as out of scope.

\subsection{CSRC, and the MOF taxonomy (geo-blocked)}
The \textbf{CSRC} is the regulator, not a repository: it mandates disclosure and delegates the public store
to CNINFO and the exchanges, so it is not itself a fetch target. The \textbf{MOF}'s CAS General-Purpose
Taxonomy --- the national-GAAP vocabulary an XBRL path would need --- is hosted at \texttt{xbrl.mof.gov.cn},
which is \textbf{geo-blocked} outside mainland China (a probe returns no response). This would block an XBRL
strategy, but it is moot here: the PDF annual reports carry Chinese line-item \emph{labels}, not taxonomy
concepts, so \texttt{EDGAR.jl} classifies by label (the dedicated \texttt{vocab\_cas.jl}) and never resolves
the MOF taxonomy at all.

\paragraph{Out of scope: SOEs and funds.} Two other Chinese disclosure channels exist but are not listed-issuer
financial statements and are outside the current scope: the \textbf{MOF}/\textbf{SASAC} channel for
state-owned-enterprise reporting (largely internal, not a public keyless store), and \textbf{AMAC}, the
fund-industry association that collects mutual- and private-fund disclosures. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} covers the CSRC listed-company universe via CNINFO.

\section{Entities covered}\label{sec:china-entities}

Because CNINFO is a single portal over all three exchanges, ``which filers can I reach?'' has a simple answer:
the \textbf{A-share listed universe} --- every company listed on the Shanghai, Shenzhen or Beijing exchange ---
plus the publicly-offered funds and REITs disclosed through the same portal (Table~\ref{tab:china-entities}). This is simpler than India, where the three channels covered
\emph{different} universes; here the \texttt{column} parameter of the discovery query selects the board, but
all three resolve through the same endpoint and the same identity scheme. A filer is identified by its
six-digit exchange code together with CNINFO's \texttt{orgId} (\texttt{EntityId(:cninfo,\ "code,orgId")}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The A-share listed universe CNINFO covers, by exchange (counts approximate, order of magnitude).}
\label{tab:china-entities}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.16\textwidth}p{0.34\textwidth}p{0.16\textwidth}p{0.18\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Exchange (\texttt{column})} & \textbf{Inclusion criterion} & \textbf{Universe (order of mag.)} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{SSE} (\texttt{sse}) & Equity listed on the Shanghai Stock Exchange --- main board plus the STAR Market & $\sim$2{,}200 issuers & \textbf{covered} via CNINFO \\
\textbf{SZSE} (\texttt{szse}) & Equity listed on the Shenzhen Stock Exchange --- main board plus ChiNext & $\sim$2{,}800 issuers & \textbf{covered} via CNINFO \\
\textbf{BSE} (\texttt{bj}) & Equity listed on the Beijing Stock Exchange (SME/innovation board) & $\sim$260 issuers & \textbf{covered} via CNINFO \\
\textbf{Funds \& REITs} (\texttt{fund}) & Publicly-offered collective vehicles disclosed through the same portal --- ETFs, LOFs, open-end funds and infrastructure REITs & $\sim$10{,}000+ funds, $\sim$60 REITs & \textbf{covered} via CNINFO \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{A-share listed issuers}
The covered set is \textbf{A-share listed companies} --- domestically listed, RMB-denominated equity issuers
filing periodic reports under CSRC/exchange rules. Roughly 5{,}400 companies in total across the three
exchanges, each publishing an audited \textbf{annual report} under
CAS. A company is reached by its six-digit code and CNINFO \texttt{orgId} --- e.g.\ Ping~An~Bank
(\texttt{000001}, \texttt{orgId gssz0000001}), Kweichow~Moutai (\texttt{600519}), Vanke (\texttt{000002}).
Dual A$+$H-listed companies (also listed in Hong~Kong) appear here through their A-share code; their separate
HKEX filing is a different repository, reached through the \textbf{Hong~Kong} chapter (Chapter~\ref{sys:hongkong}),
where the same issuer files its HKFRS accounts.

\subsection{Public funds and REITs (\texttt{column=fund})}\label{sec:china-funds}
Beyond listed \emph{companies}, CNINFO's \texttt{fund} feed carries the annual reports of publicly-offered
collective vehicles, and \texttt{EDGAR.jl} extracts these too --- they resolve through the same discovery
query (with \texttt{column\ =\ "fund"}) and the same \texttt{orgId} identity scheme, but note that they are
\emph{not} indexed by the listed-company name resolver, so they are reached by code or by browsing the fund
category rather than through \texttt{cninfo\_org}.
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Public infrastructure REITs} (\cncjk{公募REITs}{}) --- roughly 60 listed on the SSE (codes
    \texttt{508xxx}) and SZSE (\texttt{180xxx}) since 2021. A REIT \emph{consolidates} its operating
    project-companies, so it files corporate-shaped statements --- a real income statement with revenue, a
    cash-flow statement, and both \cncjk{合并}{} (consolidated) and \cncjk{个别}{} (standalone) sets --- and
    therefore rides the \emph{same} CAS extraction path as an operating company, cross-footing on the
    consolidated statements. Example: the SF~Express logistics REIT (\texttt{180305}).
  \item \textbf{Securities investment funds} --- ETFs, LOFs and open-end funds. These use \emph{fund}
    accounting: equity is net assets (\cncjk{净资产}{}, the fund's NAV) rather than shareholders' equity, the
    income statement is investment- and interest-income driven with no operating-revenue line, and no
    cash-flow statement is filed. A small dedicated label profile maps these (net-assets total to equity;
    \cncjk{投资收益}{}/\cncjk{公允价值变动收益}{} as the income components) so the balance sheet still
    cross-foots and the net result is captured. Example: an equity-index ETF's annual report.
\end{itemize}
The private-fund channel (\textbf{AMAC}) --- non-public mutual and private funds disclosed to the
asset-management association rather than to an exchange --- remains out of scope; the publicly-offered
vehicles above are reached precisely because they disclose on CNINFO like any listed issuer.

\subsection{US-listed issuers --- reachable via the SEC today (no new code)}\label{sec:china-adr}
A distinct slice of the largest Chinese companies does \emph{not} need the CNINFO/PDF path at all: those
cross-listed in the United States as \textbf{American Depositary Receipts (ADRs)} --- Alibaba, PDD, JD,
Baidu, NetEase and many more --- file \textbf{Form~20-F} (the foreign private issuer's annual report) with
the SEC, so their financials are already reachable through \texttt{EDGAR.jl}'s existing \texttt{SEC} system,
as \textbf{tagged XBRL} rather than a PDF. For those specific names that is the \emph{better} source --- the
same balance sheet, income statement and cash-flow statement arrive as classified facts with zero
China-specific code, on the \texttt{us-gaap}/\texttt{ifrs-full} vocabularies the SEC path already uses (a
20-F filer reports under IFRS or US~GAAP, not CAS). So the practical rule is: for a US-listed Chinese issuer,
reach it through the SEC (\texttt{fetch\_filing(cik, accession)}); reserve the CNINFO PDF channel for the
A-share universe that files \emph{only} in the mainland.

\paragraph{Boundary.} The scope is the \textbf{annual reports} CNINFO carries --- A-share listed issuers plus
the publicly-offered funds and REITs above (\S\ref{sec:china-funds}), with US-listed names better reached
through the SEC. B-shares and purely Hong-Kong-listed (H-share) entities, the interim/quarterly reports, and
the SOE (MOF/SASAC) and private-fund (AMAC) channels are outside the current China scope; interim reports and
the second statement types follow as later phases land. \textbf{Non-listed}
companies are effectively out of reach: the SAMR company registry (\texttt{gsxt.gov.cn}) holds every firm's
statutory annual filing but publishes little financial detail and offers no keyless API (it is CAPTCHA-gated),
and the commercial aggregators that resell it are paid --- so, as with India's MCA21 registrar, the
all-companies universe is not a practical financial-statement source. The one exception is \textbf{non-listed
bond issuers} (many large SOEs and financing vehicles), which must publish audited statements through the
bond-market disclosure channels (the interbank market via NAFMII/ChinaMoney and exchange bonds via CNINFO);
that channel is a possible later extension, not part of the current equity scope.

\section{Forms and Statement Types}\label{sec:china-forms}

Chinese listed companies disclose to the exchanges (and thus to CNINFO) under CSRC rules and the exchanges'
listing rules --- the analogue of the SEC's periodic-reporting regime. Disclosure spans a wide document
universe (Table~\ref{tab:china-forms}), of which \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets the one that carries the full
financial statements: the \textbf{annual report}, a single PDF. Unlike an Indian
Reg.~33 results filing --- a \emph{condensed} statement set (\S\ref{sec:india-statements}) --- a Chinese
annual report is the \textbf{complete annual accounts}: the four primary statements, the notes, and a
management discussion, closer in scope to a U.S.\ 10-K than to a quarterly
results table. That completeness is what makes the PDF worth the extraction cost.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The Chinese exchange-disclosure landscape (via CNINFO); \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets the annual report.}
\label{tab:china-forms}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.27\textwidth}p{0.43\textwidth}p{0.16\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Family ($\to$ \texttt{category})} & \textbf{Documents} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
Periodic reports & Annual Report, Semi-Annual Report, Quarterly Reports & \textbf{annual PDF} (extracted) \\
Issuance \& prospectuses & IPO / rights-issue / bond prospectuses & \emph{not yet} \\
Major-event announcements & material events, board \& shareholder resolutions & discovery only (planned) \\
Governance \& ownership & shareholding-change, share-pledge, buy-back announcements & \emph{not yet} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{Periodic Reports}
The annual report, semi-annual report and the first- and
third-quarter reports --- China's analogue of the 10-K/10-Q, carrying the CAS financial
statements. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets the \textbf{annual report}; every CNINFO annual-report query also
returns a paired \textbf{summary}, a shorter PDF the adapter distinguishes from the report
proper and skips. Interim (semi-annual, quarterly) reports use the same CNINFO seam and CAS shape and are a
straightforward later extension.

\subsection{Statement Types under CAS}\label{sec:china-statements}
A CAS annual report carries \textbf{four} primary statements, each typically presented \emph{twice} --- a
\textbf{consolidated} set and the \textbf{parent-company} set; \texttt{EDGAR.jl} takes the consolidated by
default, as elsewhere:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Balance sheet}: assets, liabilities and owners' equity.
  \item \textbf{Income statement}: operating revenue through net profit. (CAS titles it the ``profit
    statement'', not ``income statement''.)
  \item \textbf{Cash-flow statement}: operating, investing and financing activities (the CAS format is close
    to the IFRS indirect method).
  \item \textbf{Statement of changes in owners' equity} --- which, unlike an Indian condensed results
    filing, \emph{is} present in full.
\end{itemize}

\noindent \textbf{Sector formats.} As in India, the statement shape varies by company class: banks, insurers
and securities firms file the MOF's \emph{financial-institution} CAS formats --- a bank's balance sheet leads
with loans and advances and deposits rather than inventory and trade receivables, exactly the
Banking-Regulation shape seen at India's \texttt{BANKING\_} template. Classification (C3) must therefore
branch on sector, which in the PDF path means recognising the sector's characteristic line labels, not a
filename prefix. In all, seven CAS formats are handled and cross-foot: general-industry (in \cncjk{元}{}/
\cncjk{千元}{}), the combined \cncjk{合并及公司}{} layout, bank, insurer, securities/brokerage, REIT (which
consolidates its project-companies onto the corporate statements), and the securities investment fund
(net-asset accounting, \S\ref{sec:china-funds}).

\subsection{Fund Portfolios (\cncjk{投资组合报告}{})}\label{sec:china-fund-portfolio}
A public fund's annual report carries something a company's does not: a \textbf{portfolio report} (\S8,
\cncjk{投资组合报告}{}) --- the fund's \emph{holdings}. This is the CNINFO analogue of the SEC's N-PORT
(\S\ref{N-PORT}), and it is a different artefact from the financial statements: a list of \emph{positions},
not classified statement facts. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} extracts it with a dedicated function separate from the CAS
statement path:
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Julia]
pf = extract_fund_portfolio(path)   # -> FundPortfolio
pf.holdings                         # Vector{FundHolding}: category, code, name, quantity, fair_value, pct_nav
pf.asset_composition                # 8.1: assets by class (equity / fixed income / cash), amount + % of assets
pf.industry                         # 8.2: equity exposure by CSRC sector, summed across index/active sub-books
\end{lstlisting}
The \S8 pages are located from the PDF outline and parsed with Docling; tables are classified by \emph{shape}
(so multi-page holdings continuations, which carry no repeated title, still parse). A useful internal check:
the holdings' fair values sum to the balance sheet's stock line (on the validation ETF, \cncjk{股票}{}
$=$ RMB~838.76\,M, matched to rounding by the 79 extracted positions).

\paragraph{Coverage is uneven by security type --- by regulation, not omission.} The equity schedule (\S8.3)
lists \textbf{every} stock position, but bonds, asset-backed securities, precious metals and warrants
(\S8.6--8.9) are disclosed only as the \textbf{top five} by fair value. So for a bond fund \texttt{holdings}
is the top-5 bonds, \emph{not} the full book --- the fund-wide fixed-income total lives in
\texttt{asset\_composition} instead. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} extracts what is disclosed and does not silently imply
completeness where the filing itself stops at five. A \textbf{REIT} has no such section: it holds operating
infrastructure projects, not securities, so its \S8 is an operating-management report and its ``portfolio''
is per-project operating and valuation data --- a separate artefact, not reached by this function.

\section{Taxonomy: CAS Labels, Not Concepts}\label{sec:china-taxonomy}

China inverts the taxonomy story of every prior system, and this is the chapter's core technical point.
DART needed \emph{no} vocabulary because K-IFRS tags bare \texttt{ifrs-full} (\S\ref{sec:dart-kifrs}); India
needed a vocabulary keyed on a national \emph{namespace} (\texttt{in-bse-fin}, \S\ref{sec:india-taxonomy}).
China has \textbf{no namespace to key on at all}: the retrieval artefact is a PDF, and a PDF line carries a
Chinese \textbf{label} (\cncjk{资产总计}{}, ``Total assets''), not a namespaced concept
(\texttt{ifrs-full:Assets}). The classification key is the \emph{human label}.

There \emph{is} a national vocabulary --- the MOF's \textbf{CAS General-Purpose Taxonomy}, an
XBRL-International-acknowledged taxonomy at
\texttt{xbrl.mof.gov.cn} --- but it is doubly out of the picture: the host is \textbf{geo-blocked} outside
mainland China (\S\ref{sec:china-repos}), \emph{and} it never appears in the PDFs the general filer universe
publishes. So \texttt{EDGAR.jl} does not resolve it. Instead, China is classified through a dedicated
\textbf{\texttt{vocab\_cas.jl}} keyed on the CAS \textbf{Chinese labels} --- the label-first analogue of the
by-namespace national vocabularies, and the natural fit for the PDF extractor's document-first path, whose
facts already anchor on line-item \emph{text} rather than concept URIs (Chapter~\ref{ch:pdf}). The anchor
labels are stable across filers because the MOF prescribes the statement line items
(Table~\ref{tab:china-cas-anchors}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{CAS statement anchors --- the Chinese labels \texttt{vocab\_cas.jl} maps to canonical roles (a
representative subset; general-industry format).}
\label{tab:china-cas-anchors}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.30\textwidth}p{0.34\textwidth}p{0.28\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{CAS label} & \textbf{English} & \textbf{Statement --- role} \\
\midrule
\cncjk{资产总计}{} & Total assets & Balance sheet --- Assets \\
\cncjk{负债合计}{} & Total liabilities & Balance sheet --- Liabilities \\
\cncjk{所有者权益（或股东权益）合计}{} & Total owners'/shareholders' equity & Balance sheet --- Equity \\
\cncjk{营业收入}{} & Operating revenue & Income statement --- Revenue \\
\cncjk{营业利润}{} & Operating profit & Income statement \\
\cncjk{利润总额}{} & Total profit (pre-tax) & Income statement \\
\cncjk{净利润}{} & Net profit & Income statement --- NetIncome \\
\cncjk{归属于母公司所有者的净利润}{} & Net profit attributable to owners of the parent & Income statement --- net income (parent) \\
\cncjk{经营活动产生的现金流量净额}{} & Net cash flow from operating activities & Cash-flow --- OperatingCashFlow \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{CAS versus IFRS.} CAS/ASBE has been substantially \textbf{IFRS-converged}
since the 2007 ASBE, so the statement structure and most recognition/measurement rules read like IFRS ---
which is why the anchor labels above map cleanly onto the same canonical roles the \texttt{ifrs-full}
vocabulary uses. A few deliberate divergences persist (they matter for a validation oracle, not for
classification): CAS \textbf{prohibits reversing impairment losses} on long-lived assets (IFRS permits it),
narrows some \textbf{related-party} disclosures for state-controlled entities, and prescribes MOF statement
\emph{formats} more rigidly than IAS~1. Figures are in \textbf{renminbi} (RMB), with the
unit (yuan, or in ten-thousands / thousands) stated in the statement header --- a
locale/unit the PDF extractor's amount parser must read, the CAS counterpart of India's ``Crores''
rounding.

\medskip\noindent One consequence worth flagging early. Chinese filers headline the \textbf{parent-attributable}
net profit rather than \emph{total} net profit, so a value-against-value
check against a third-party source (which usually reports the parent figure) must pick the right line --- the
same net-income-attribution subtlety documented for India's NSE filings (\S\ref{sec:india-yahoo}). The CAS
oracle (C4) reconciles on the parent line.

\section{Retrieving Filings}\label{sec:china-retrieve}

Discovery is \textbf{keyless} --- a browser \texttt{User-Agent} and a CNINFO \texttt{Referer}, no API key and
(unlike NSE) no cookie bootstrap. The API mirrors every other system, with the one difference that
the fetched \texttt{Filing} is a \textbf{PDF} whose \texttt{facts} route through the \textbf{Docling} PDF
extractor rather than the XBRL path:

\begin{lstlisting}
hs = discover(CNINFOFilings(); code = "000001")   # Ping An Bank; annual reports (the default)
f  = fetch_filing(first(hs))                       # newest; downloads the CAS annual-report PDF
facts(f)                                            # statements via the PDF extractor + vocab_cas
                                                    # narratives(f) -> the MD&A / notes prose
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent A few notes:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{No key, no cookie.} CNINFO's JSON endpoints answer any browser-headed client; there is nothing
    to configure --- China, like India, needs no credential, and unlike India needs no session bootstrap.
  \item \textbf{By code or name.} Discovery keys off the six-digit exchange \texttt{code}
    (e.g.\ \texttt{"000001"}) or a company name, resolved to its \texttt{orgId} live via CNINFO's
    \texttt{topSearch} --- so, unlike DART, no entity index is downloaded. The board is selected by the
    \texttt{column} keyword (\texttt{szse} / \texttt{sse} / \texttt{bj}), defaulting to auto-detect from the
    code range.
  \item \textbf{Handles, newest first.} Each \texttt{FilingHandle}'s identity is the packed
    \texttt{EntityId(:cninfo,\ "code,orgId")} and its \texttt{period\_end} the fiscal-year end (Chinese fiscal
    years end 31~December), so \texttt{first(hs)} is the most recent annual report; the paired summary
    is filtered out.
  \item \textbf{Same caller API, PDF underneath.} \texttt{fetch\_filing(h)} $\to$ \texttt{facts(f)} /
    \texttt{narratives(f)} $\to$ \texttt{select\_statement} is identical to SEC, DART or India for the caller;
    what differs is \emph{internally} --- \texttt{facts(f)} runs the PDF extractor (Chapter~\ref{ch:pdf}) with
    \texttt{vocab\_cas} label classification, not an XBRL parse. A cheap \texttt{pdf.js} pre-scan locates the
    consolidated-statements page range (TOC bookmarks, falling back to headings) and the fiscal year;
    \textbf{Docling} then parses just those \(\sim\)15 pages, and the facts are derived from its table cells
    --- consolidated statements kept, parent-company (母公司) copies dropped, amounts scaled by the page's own
    单位 (元/千元/万元/百万元). Verified to cross-foot (Assets $=$ Liabilities $+$ Equity) and close the
    cash-flow identity on general-industry, combined-column (合并及公司) and bank filers. Born-digital annual
    reports (the norm; the text layer extracts CJK directly) need no OCR; older or scanned filings fall back
    to the extractor's OCR tier.
\end{itemize}

\chapter{Hong Kong: HKEXnews}
\label{sys:hongkong}

Hong Kong is being added as the \textbf{eighth} \texttt{FilingSystem}, and it sits closest to China: both are
\textbf{PDF-native} --- a filing's facts come from an annual-report PDF, not an XBRL package --- but Hong~Kong
inverts China's hardest problem. Its reports are in \textbf{English} and prepared under \textbf{HKFRS}, which
is substantially identical to IFRS, so classification reuses the shared \texttt{ifrs-full} concepts through a
light English label map rather than a national-language vocabulary (no CJK \texttt{vocab\_cas} analogue is
needed). As with China, the chapter opens with a \emph{Repositories} survey (\S\ref{sec:hk-repos}): the
question is which door to build on, and the answer is \textbf{HKEXnews}, the designated disclosure portal,
whose keyless JSON API resolves an issuer and lists its annual-report PDFs. Identity is the five-digit
\textbf{stock code} paired with HKEXnews's internal \texttt{stockId} (\texttt{EntityId(:hkex, "00700")}),
resolved from a bare code or a company name.

\emph{Status:} \textbf{planned} --- recon complete, implementation (HK0--HK4) pending. Extraction will try the
Tier-1 (\texttt{pdf.js}) path first --- English born-digital statements are far simpler geometry than CJK ---
falling back to Docling only where the tables demand it, and map the headline English labels to
\texttt{ifrs-full} concepts.

\section{Repositories}\label{sec:hk-repos}

Like mainland China, Hong~Kong has \emph{no} ``Hong Kong EDGAR'' in the sense of a regulator's own document
store. Instead one exchange-run portal, \textbf{HKEXnews}, carries the entire listed universe's disclosures;
everything else is either a delegating regulator, a paid registry that holds no public accounts, or the SEC
for names that also list in the United States (Table~\ref{tab:hk-repos}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{Hong Kong disclosure channels; \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets HKEXnews.}
\label{tab:hk-repos}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.19\textwidth}p{0.35\textwidth}p{0.17\textwidth}p{0.17\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Channel} & \textbf{What it holds} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{HKEXnews} & Every listed-issuer filing (Main Board \& GEM) --- annual/interim reports, results
  announcements, circulars, ESG --- as English HKFRS PDFs & \textbf{Keyless} JSON API & \textbf{primary
  channel} \\
\textbf{SFC} & The securities regulator: licensing and enforcement registers & Public, but \emph{no}
  financial statements & regulator --- not a store \\
\textbf{Companies Registry} & Every HK-incorporated company's annual return (NAR1 --- \emph{no} accounts);
  private-company accounts are not filed publicly & \textbf{Paid} (ICRIS / e-Search), per document & out of
  scope (no public financials) \\
\textbf{US SEC} & Secondary / dual-US-listed names (Alibaba, \ldots) --- Form 20-F as tagged XBRL & Keyless
  (the existing \texttt{SEC} system) & via \texttt{SEC} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{HKEXnews --- the one door.} Operated by \textbf{HKEX}, \texttt{www1.hkexnews.hk} is the designated
portal for every listed-issuer regulatory filing on both the \textbf{Main Board} and \textbf{GEM}: annual
reports, interim reports, results announcements, circulars and ESG reports. Financial statements arrive as
\textbf{English PDFs} under HKFRS. It is HK's CNINFO --- one portal spanning the whole listed universe --- and
it is \textbf{keyless}: a \texttt{prefix.do} endpoint resolves a company name or code to an internal
\texttt{stockId}, and a \texttt{titleSearchServlet.do} endpoint lists an issuer's documents (each with a
direct PDF link), so discovery and retrieval need no account or key. This is the channel \texttt{EDGAR.jl}
builds on.

\paragraph{SFC --- regulator, not a store.} The \textbf{Securities and Futures Commission} mandates disclosure
and licenses intermediaries, but delegates the public filing store to HKEX/HKEXnews --- exactly as the CSRC
delegates to CNINFO. Its own public registers concern licensing and enforcement, not financial statements, so
it is not a fetch target.

\paragraph{Companies Registry --- all companies, no public accounts.} The statutory registry
(\texttt{www.cr.gov.hk}) is Hong~Kong's Companies-House / SAMR analogue: it holds every HK-incorporated
company's \textbf{annual return} (form NAR1, which carries \emph{no} financial statements), and private
companies are not required to file accounts publicly. Access is \textbf{paid}, per document (ICRIS / e-Search).
So, as with SAMR (China) and MCA21 (India), the all-companies universe is not a practical financial-statement
source; only listed issuers --- through HKEXnews --- are reachable.

\paragraph{US-listed names --- reach via the SEC.} HK-listed companies that \emph{also} list in the United
States (Alibaba \texttt{09988}\,/\,\texttt{BABA} and other dual/ADR names) file \textbf{Form 20-F} with the SEC
as tagged XBRL --- the better source for those specific names, already reachable through \texttt{EDGAR.jl}'s
\texttt{SEC} system. The rule mirrors the China-ADR rule: US-listed $\to$ SEC; the HKEXnews PDF channel is for
HK-primary filers.

\section{Entities Covered}\label{sec:hk-entities}

The reachable universe is the \textbf{HKEX-listed universe} --- every company listed on the \textbf{Main
Board} or \textbf{GEM} (Table~\ref{tab:hk-entities}) --- because, whatever an issuer's origin, it files the
\emph{same} English HKFRS/IFRS annual report on HKEXnews. Roughly 2{,}600 issuers in total. A filer is reached
by its five-digit stock code and HKEXnews \texttt{stockId}.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The HKEX-listed universe covered via HKEXnews, by board (counts approximate, order of magnitude).}
\label{tab:hk-entities}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.15\textwidth}p{0.44\textwidth}p{0.14\textwidth}p{0.16\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Board} & \textbf{Inclusion criterion} & \textbf{Universe (order of mag.)} & \textbf{In
\texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{Main Board} & Established issuers meeting the profit / market-cap / revenue tests --- local~HK,
  mainland (H-share, red-chip, P-chip) and foreign companies alike & $\sim$2{,}300 issuers & \textbf{covered}
  via HKEXnews \\
\textbf{GEM} & The growth / SME board --- smaller or earlier-stage issuers (stock codes \texttt{08xxx}) &
  $\sim$300 issuers & \textbf{covered} via HKEXnews \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{By issuer origin}
Hong~Kong's listed companies are conventionally grouped by \emph{where they are incorporated and controlled}
--- a distinction that matters for market context but \textbf{not} for extraction, since all file one
English, IFRS-family annual report on the same portal:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item \textbf{Local HK issuers} --- incorporated and operating in Hong~Kong (the banks, property developers
    and utilities that anchor the Hang~Seng).
  \item \textbf{H-shares} (\cncjk{H股}{}) --- \emph{mainland}-incorporated companies listed in Hong~Kong (the
    large state banks and industrials); very often the H counter of a \textbf{dual A$+$H} listing, whose A
    counter is reached instead through CNINFO (Chapter~\ref{sys:china}).
  \item \textbf{Red chips} (\cncjk{红筹}{}) --- incorporated \emph{outside} the mainland (Bermuda/Cayman/HK)
    but mainland-\emph{state}-controlled, with principal business on the mainland (China~Mobile, CITIC).
  \item \textbf{P-chips} --- offshore-incorporated but mainland \emph{private}-sector; most of the HK-listed
    technology names, including Tencent (\texttt{00700}), sit here.
  \item \textbf{Foreign issuers} --- non-Greater-China companies listed under Chapter~19 (Prada, Samsonite,
    L'Occitane).
\end{itemize}
The extractor treats all of these identically: each publishes a consolidated HKFRS/IFRS statement set in
English, classified by the same \texttt{ifrs-full} label map.

\subsection{Secondary and dual listings --- reach via the SEC}
A slice of the largest names carry a \textbf{secondary} or \textbf{dual-primary} listing in Hong~Kong under
Chapter~19C while being \emph{primarily} listed in the United States --- Alibaba (\texttt{09988}), JD,
NetEase, Baidu and others. For these the SEC's \textbf{Form~20-F} (tagged XBRL) is the better source, reached
through the existing \texttt{SEC} system with no HK-specific code, so \texttt{EDGAR.jl} routes them there; the
HKEXnews PDF channel is reserved for HK-primary filers (\S\ref{sec:hk-repos}).

\paragraph{Accounting standards.} \textbf{HKFRS} is the default and \textbf{IFRS} is accepted as equivalent
--- the face statements are identical, so the English label\,$\to$\,\texttt{ifrs-full} map covers the vast
majority. A few issuers report under \textbf{US~GAAP} (certain secondary-listed names) or, for some
mainland-incorporated issuers, \textbf{CASBE} with reconciliation; those are edge cases whose face labels
differ from the IFRS set.

\subsection{REITs, business trusts and investment funds}\label{sec:hk-reits}

Hong~Kong's \textbf{REITs} (Link~REIT \texttt{00823}, Fortune~REIT \texttt{00778}, Sunlight~REIT \texttt{00435})
and business trusts list and file on HKEXnews like any other issuer --- reached by the same
\texttt{discover(HKEXFilings())} --- but their accounts take a \emph{net-asset} shape: under IAS~32 the units
are puttable, so the balance sheet ends not in ``total equity'' but in \textbf{net assets attributable to
Unitholders} (the residual), above a \textbf{total liabilities, excluding net assets attributable to
Unitholders} line, and the income statement's bottom line is a \textbf{(loss)/profit for the year, before
transactions with Unitholders}. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} maps these unit-holder lines to the same \texttt{ifrs-full}
anchors (net assets attributable to unitholders $\to$ the equity-equivalent \texttt{Equity}; the
excluding-units liabilities $\to$ \texttt{Liabilities}; the pre-transactions result $\to$ \texttt{ProfitLoss}),
so a REIT's revenue, result and balance sheet extract through the ordinary path --- validated on Link~REIT (FY
ending 31~March; property revaluations can push the year to a loss). Its \textbf{property portfolio} is the REIT
analogue of a fund's holdings: \texttt{extract\_reit\_portfolio} recovers the by-segment valuation breakdown
(Link~REIT: Destination / Community / Neighbourhood, each with its valuation and share of the portfolio) into
the \emph{same} \texttt{FundPortfolio} type \texttt{extract\_fund\_portfolio} returns for China and India
(\S\ref{sec:india-formats}), so funds and REITs share one portfolio API. The \emph{distribution} statement is a
further REIT-specific extension.

\begin{lstlisting}
using EDGAR, PythonCall           # PythonCall loads the Docling extension the PDF extractor uses

# fetch the REIT's newest annual report, then read its property-portfolio composition
hs = discover(HKEXFilings(); code = "00823")   # Link REIT (SEHK: 00823), newest first
f  = fetch_filing(first(hs))                    # -> a :pdf Filing; f.content is the on-disk path
pf = extract_reit_portfolio(f.content)          # -> FundPortfolio (the same type funds return)

total = sum(a.amount for a in pf.asset_composition)
for a in pf.asset_composition                   # asset_composition :: Vector{FundAssetClass}
    println(rpad(a.item, 18), lpad("HK\$$(round(a.amount/1e9, digits=1))bn", 12),
            lpad("$(a.pct_total_assets)%", 10))
end
println("Total portfolio  HK\$$(round(total/1e9, digits=1))bn")
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{lstlisting}
Destination          HK$21.8bn     19.9%
Community            HK$61.4bn     56.0%
Neighbourhood        HK$26.3bn     24.1%
Total portfolio  HK$109.5bn
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent \texttt{extract\_reit\_portfolio(f.content)} returns a \texttt{FundPortfolio} --- identical to what
\texttt{extract\_fund\_portfolio} gives for a Chinese or Indian fund --- so \texttt{pf.asset\_composition} is a
\texttt{Vector\{FundAssetClass\}} with \texttt{item} (the segment), \texttt{amount} (the valuation, in full
units) and \texttt{pct\_total\_assets} (its share of the portfolio). Swap the code for another REIT
(Fortune~\texttt{00778}, Sunlight~\texttt{00435}) and the same three lines work; with the PDF already on disk,
call \texttt{extract\_reit\_portfolio("path/to.pdf")} directly.

\paragraph{Mutual-fund holdings --- the SFC gap.} A REIT's portfolio is on HKEXnews (in the annual report); a
\emph{mutual} fund's is not. Hong~Kong's fund holdings sit in each fund's annual / semi-annual report on its
\emph{manager's} website --- there is \textbf{no central, keyless repository}, unlike India's NSE feed or
China's CNINFO portfolio-report filing. The SFC \emph{authorises} products and publishes a \textbf{register}
(fund name, manager, type, ISIN, authorisation status --- its ``List of publicly offered investment products''
and ``List of Registered OFCs''), but \emph{not} holdings. So the India/China ``discover the portfolio filing''
path has no HK analogue. Three ways forward, the choice \textbf{deferred} for now:
\begin{enumerate}[nosep]
  \item \textbf{Extract holdings from a fund's report PDF} --- the strong option, because it reuses everything.
    The holdings \emph{are} in each fund's annual / semi-annual report; we simply cannot \emph{discover} them
    centrally. Supply the PDF (or fetch it as a \texttt{Generic} filing, Chapter~\ref{ch:generic}) and a
    portfolio parser --- \texttt{extract\_reit\_portfolio} / \texttt{extract\_fund\_portfolio} --- recovers the
    holdings into a \texttt{FundPortfolio}. Discovery is blocked; extraction is not.
  \item \textbf{An SFC fund-register lookup} --- a \texttt{discover}-style metadata search over the SFC register
    (fund name $\to$ manager, ISIN, type, authorisation). Useful for \emph{finding} funds, but it returns no
    holdings.
  \item \textbf{HKEX-listed ETF daily PCFs} --- structured holdings (portfolio composition files), but scattered
    on each manager's site: per-manager adapters, fragile.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Boundary.} The scope is \textbf{listed-issuer annual and interim reports} on HKEXnews (Main Board
and GEM) --- including REITs and business trusts (\S\ref{sec:hk-reits}) --- with US-primary names better
reached through the SEC. \textbf{Non-listed} Hong~Kong companies are out of reach: the Companies Registry holds
only the accounts-free annual return and charges per document (\S\ref{sec:hk-repos}).

\section{Forms and Statement Types}\label{sec:hk-forms}

Hong~Kong issuers disclose to HKEXnews under the SEHK \textbf{Listing Rules} --- the analogue of the SEC's
periodic-reporting regime. The document universe is wide (annual and interim reports, results announcements,
monthly returns, next-day disclosure returns, circulars, proxy forms, ESG reports), of which \texttt{EDGAR.jl}
targets the one that carries the full financial statements: the \textbf{annual report}, a single English PDF
filed under the ``Financial Statements/ESG Information'' headline category. Like a Chinese CAS annual report
--- and unlike an Indian condensed Reg.~33 results filing --- it is the \textbf{complete annual accounts}: the
primary HKFRS statements (statement of financial position, statement of profit or loss and other
comprehensive income, statement of cash flows, statement of changes in equity), the notes, and a management
discussion, closer in scope to a U.S.\ 10-K.

\paragraph{PDF only --- no XBRL.} The decisive point for retrieval is what Hong~Kong \emph{does not} mandate.
Every prior \texttt{FilingSystem} except China exposes an XBRL or iXBRL package as its keyless primary channel
--- the SEC, ESEF, Companies House, EDINET and DART all tag their financial statements at source.
\textbf{Hong~Kong does not}: HKEXnews serves the financial statements only as \textbf{PDF}, with no
structured-reporting requirement for issuer accounts (a scan of an issuer's recent filings returns
\texttt{FILE\_TYPE = PDF} for every document type). So Hong~Kong is, like China, \textbf{PDF-native}, and
\texttt{facts(f)} flows through the shared PDF extractor (Chapter~\ref{ch:pdf}); the difference from China is
only \emph{language} (English) and \emph{standard} (HKFRS, an IFRS-converged framework whose face statements
match \texttt{ifrs-full}). The one structured twin is \emph{external}: where an issuer also lists in the
United States, its SEC \textbf{Form~20-F} carries the same statements as tagged iXBRL --- which is why
\texttt{EDGAR.jl} routes those dual/secondary names to the \texttt{SEC} path (\S\ref{sec:hk-entities}).

Table~\ref{tab:hk-forms} surveys the HKEXnews document universe by headline category (the
\texttt{titleSearchServlet.do} search filters on these). \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets the \textbf{annual report};
the \textbf{interim / half-year report} and the \textbf{results announcements} are the natural extensions on
the same seam.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The HKEXnews document landscape (every document is a PDF); \texttt{EDGAR.jl} targets the annual
report.}
\label{tab:hk-forms}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.27\textwidth}p{0.42\textwidth}p{0.19\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Headline category} & \textbf{Documents (Tier-2 subtypes)} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{Financial Statements / ESG Information} & \textbf{Annual Report}; Interim / Half-Year Report; ESG
  Report & \textbf{annual PDF} (target); interim (extension); ESG $\to$ \texttt{narratives} \\
\textbf{Announcements and Notices} & Annual / Interim / Quarterly \emph{results} announcements (a condensed
  version of the face statements, published earlier); plus dividends, board-meeting dates, poll results,
  inside information, share-scheme grants, directorate changes & results announcements (extension); the rest
  not targeted \\
\textbf{Circulars} & General mandates, AGM and M\&A circulars & not targeted \\
\textbf{Proxy Forms \& Meeting Notices} & Forms of proxy, notices of AGM / EGM & not targeted \\
\textbf{Constitutional Documents} & Memorandum \& Articles of Association & not targeted \\
\textbf{Monthly Returns} & Movements in securities (monthly share count) & not targeted \\
\textbf{Next Day Disclosure Returns} & Share buybacks / capital changes (by volume, the most frequent type) &
  not targeted \\
\textbf{Debt and Structured Products} & MTN-programme notes, offering circulars, pricing supplements & not
  targeted \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent The table above is organised by HKEXnews's \emph{own} headline categories; for extraction what
matters is a different axis --- which documents carry \emph{accounts}. Three groups.

\subsection{Financial-statement-bearing forms}\label{sec:hk-forms-financial}
The extraction candidates --- all English PDF, all mapping to \texttt{ifrs-full}:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item \textbf{Annual Report} --- the target: the complete annual HKFRS accounts (retrieved as
    \S\ref{sec:hk-retrieve}).
  \item \textbf{Interim / Half-Year Report} --- the full mid-year accounts, under \emph{Financial Statements /
    ESG Information}; the natural first extension --- same seam and extractor as the annual report.
  \item \textbf{Results announcements} --- the \emph{Annual} and \emph{Interim Results} announcements (under
    \emph{Announcements and Notices}) carry the same face statements in \emph{condensed} form, weeks ahead of
    the full report; the same extractor serves them, the difference being completeness, not shape.
  \item \textbf{Quarterly results} --- only \emph{some} issuers report quarterly (Tencent, US-style); most HK
    issuers report half-yearly, so quarterlies are not universal.
  \item \textbf{ESG Report} --- non-financial but narrative-rich: a \texttt{narratives} target, not
    \texttt{facts}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Governance and corporate-action filings}\label{sec:hk-forms-governance}
Structured disclosures that carry \emph{no} accounts --- not targeted:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item \textbf{Announcements and Notices} --- the catch-all: dividends, board-meeting dates, poll results,
    inside information, share-scheme grants, directorate changes, connected transactions.
  \item \textbf{Circulars} --- general mandates, AGM / M\&A circulars.
  \item \textbf{Proxy forms} and \textbf{notices of AGM / EGM}.
  \item \textbf{Constitutional Documents} --- Memorandum \& Articles of Association.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Market and registry filings}\label{sec:hk-forms-registry}
Also structured, also without accounts --- not targeted:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
  \item \textbf{Monthly Returns} --- ``Movements in Securities'' (the monthly share count).
  \item \textbf{Next Day Disclosure Returns} --- share buybacks / capital changes; by volume the most frequent
    document type (500+ for an active issuer such as Tencent).
  \item \textbf{Debt and Structured Products} --- MTN-programme notes, offering circulars, pricing supplements.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{The discovery lever.} \texttt{titleSearchServlet.do} filters on the headline \emph{category} plus the
Tier-1/Tier-2 subtype codes (\texttt{t2Gcode}\,/\,\texttt{t2code}), or on a free title-text search. HK1 discovery
(\S\ref{sec:hk-retrieve}) uses a \texttt{title\ =\ "Annual Report"} text match (\texttt{searchType\ =\ 1}),
which resolves the annual report cleanly; the robust production filter is the \emph{Financial Statements / ESG
Information}\,$\to$\,[Annual Report] category codes, to be mapped as the interim and results-announcement
extensions land.

\section{Retrieving filings}\label{sec:hk-retrieve}

Discovery is \textbf{keyless} --- HKEXnews needs no key and no cookie bootstrap. \texttt{discover(HKEXFilings();\
code=\ldots)} lists an issuer's annual reports by its five-digit stock \texttt{code} (or company \texttt{name});
each returned \texttt{FilingHandle} carries the annual-report \textbf{PDF} URL, which \texttt{fetch\_filing}
downloads into the same \texttt{Filing} every other system produces. Fetching BOC Hong Kong's 2025 annual report
is three lines:

\begin{lstlisting}
julia> using EDGAR

julia> hs = discover(HKEXFilings(); code = "02388")   # BOC Hong Kong (SEHK 2388), newest first
4-element Vector{FilingHandle}:
 FilingHandle(HKEX, hkex:02388, 2025-12-31 [HK])      # the FY2025 annual report
 FilingHandle(HKEX, hkex:02388, 2024-12-31 [HK])
 ...

julia> f = fetch_filing(first(hs))                    # FY2025 -> downloads the English HKFRS PDF

julia> facts(f)                                       # consolidated statements as ifrs-full facts
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent A few notes:

\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Resolve by code, not name.} The five-digit \texttt{code} (\texttt{"02388"}; a bare
    \texttt{"2388"} is zero-padded) is the reliable key --- it is matched \emph{exactly} against HKEXnews's
    \texttt{prefix.do} issuer index. A \texttt{name} is accepted too but the portal's autocomplete is fuzzy
    (a plain \texttt{"Hang Seng"} surfaces the tracker ETFs, not the bank), so prefer the code when you know it.
  \item \textbf{Handles, newest first.} Each handle's identity is the stock code
    (\texttt{EntityId(:hkex,\ "02388")}) and its \texttt{period\_end} the fiscal year-end parsed from the
    report title, so \texttt{first(hs)} is the most recent; pick another year by filtering on
    \texttt{period\_end}. The date window defaults to the last \texttt{years\ =\ 8}; widen or set
    \texttt{from}/\texttt{to} for older reports.
  \item \textbf{PDF-native, same API as everywhere.} Hong Kong publishes no XBRL --- the report is an English
    HKFRS \textbf{PDF}, so \texttt{fetch\_filing} returns a \texttt{:pdf} \texttt{Filing} and \texttt{facts(f)}
    runs it through the shared English-PDF extractor (\texttt{extract\_ifrs\_pdf}, the same path UK Companies
    House uses), recovering the consolidated statements onto \texttt{ifrs-full} concepts. From there
    \texttt{facts(f)} / \texttt{select\_statement} / the external-data benchmark are identical to SEC, ESEF or
    DART --- nothing about a Hong Kong filing differs for the caller. The exact fiscal date, presentation
    currency (HK\$, or a bank's abbreviated ``HK\$'m'') and scale are read from the statements themselves at
    extraction.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Worked example: from PDF to spreadsheet}\label{sec:hk-workflow}

One end-to-end pass ties the whole pipeline together --- fetch a Hong~Kong half-year report, parse it with
Docling, pull one statement, land it in DuckDB, and export it --- through the generic, source-agnostic
interface (Xiaomi, \texttt{01810}):

\begin{lstlisting}
using EDGAR

# (a) fetch the half-year report — interim discovery, then download the PDF
hs = discover(HKEXFilings(); code = "01810", report = :interim)   # Xiaomi interim reports, newest first
f  = fetch_filing(first(hs))                                      # downloads the PDF; f.content is the path
cp(f.content, "xiaomi_interim.pdf"; force = true)                 # (c) keep the PDF

# (b) send it to Docling — just the statement pages (locate them in the outline with toc(f))
sd = structured_document("xiaomi_interim.pdf"; pages = (57, 66))  # -> a StructuredDocument
write_structured_document(sd, "xiaomi_interim.sd.json")           # (c) persist the SD (reload later, no Docling)

# (d) analyse: the income statement, on ifrs-full concepts, from the already-parsed SD
rows = extract_ifrs_pdf(sd)                                       # facts from the SD — no second parse
is   = select_statement(rows, "IncomeStatement")                 # the six-month (year-to-date) figures

# (e) store just the income statement in DuckDB — EDGAR's generic sink
using DuckDB, DBInterface        # DBInterface: the generic DB API DuckDB.jl implements (EDGAR uses it too)
to_duckdb(is, "xiaomi.duckdb"; table = "income_statement")

# (f) export the table — DuckDB writes CSV and Excel natively, through the generic DBInterface
con = DBInterface.connect(DuckDB.DB, "xiaomi.duckdb")
DBInterface.execute(con, "COPY income_statement TO 'income_statement.csv' (HEADER, FORMAT CSV)")
DBInterface.execute(con, "INSTALL excel"); DBInterface.execute(con, "LOAD excel")
DBInterface.execute(con, "COPY income_statement TO 'income_statement.xlsx' WITH (FORMAT xlsx)")

# (g) the narrative layer — the simplest analysis: word-count a section
nar   = narratives(sd)
words = length(split(nar[1].text))
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent Every step is jurisdiction-agnostic: swap the first line for a \texttt{China}, \texttt{SEC} or
\texttt{Generic} fetch and the rest is unchanged. \texttt{extract\_ifrs\_pdf(sd)} takes the \emph{already-parsed}
\texttt{StructuredDocument}, so the PDF is sent to Docling exactly once and its facts \emph{and} narratives come
from the one parse. \texttt{to\_duckdb} is EDGAR's own DuckDB sink (the package extension loads automatically
when \texttt{DuckDB} is in the session), and the CSV / Excel exports are DuckDB's native \texttt{COPY}, issued
through the generic \texttt{DBInterface} --- so no writer beyond DuckDB itself is needed. (The interim income
statement prints \emph{both} a three-month and a six-month column per year; \texttt{select\_statement} returns
the six-month, year-to-date figures --- see \S\ref{sec:hk-forms-financial}.)

\chapter{India: NSE, BSE and MCA21}
\label{sys:india}

India is being added as the \textbf{sixth} \texttt{FilingSystem}. Unlike the single national regulator-API
of EDINET or DART, Indian financial disclosure is \textbf{multi-channel}: the same company's numbers surface
through the corporate registrar (\textbf{MCA21}) and through every stock exchange the company lists on
(\textbf{NSE}, \textbf{BSE}). Which channel to build on is therefore the central design decision for the
adapter --- so this chapter opens, unusually, with a \emph{Repositories} survey (\S\ref{sec:india-repos}).
Listed-company financials are filed in \textbf{Ind-AS} --- the IFRS-converged Indian Accounting Standards ---
under a national XBRL taxonomy published by the exchanges, and classify through a \textbf{dedicated}
\texttt{vocab\_indas.jl} keyed on the \texttt{in-bse-fin} namespace (the same by-namespace national-vocabulary
pattern as Japan and the UK; \S\ref{sec:india-taxonomy}). Identity is the company's \textbf{ISIN}
(\texttt{EntityId(:isin,\ "INE002A01018")}), with the NSE ticker symbol and BSE scrip code as secondary
schemes.

\emph{Status:} the seam (I0), the \textbf{offline parse} (I1) and \textbf{live NSE discovery} (I2) are
\textbf{implemented and tested}. \texttt{discover(NSEFilings();\ symbol=\ldots)} lists a company's filings
and \texttt{fetch\_filing} downloads the Ind-AS XBRL (\S\ref{sec:india-retrieve}); \texttt{facts(f)} extracts
its Ind-AS facts and \texttt{narratives(f)} its section text --- verified end-to-end against real filings
(Reliance, TCS, Maruti, \ldots). The dedicated \texttt{vocab\_indas.jl} \emph{classification} (I3) and
human-readable \emph{labels} plus the same-document BSE oracle (I4) are the remaining phases. This
\textbf{supersedes} the earlier ``India MCA21 --- paid'' note in the portability survey: MCA21 (the
registrar) is indeed paid, but that is the \emph{wrong} channel --- the \textbf{exchanges publish the
results XBRL keyless}.

\paragraph{Facts \emph{and} section text.} An NSE filing yields both. \texttt{facts(f)} returns the numeric
Ind-AS statements (the \texttt{in-bse-fin} concepts); \texttt{narratives(f)} (\S\ref{sec:api-narratives})
returns the filing's \textbf{explanatory text-blocks} --- the Regulation~33 notes tagged with the
\texttt{\ldots{}ExplanatoryTextBlock} suffix --- with \textbf{no India-specific code}, because the shared
text layer already recognises that suffix (exactly as for ESEF and EDINET). Reliance's FY2023--24 filing
exposes three: \emph{Notes on Financial Results}, \emph{Notes on Assets and Liabilities} and \emph{Notes on
Segments} (e.g.\ ``Q4~FY24 Segment Revenue includes Rs~24{,}119~crore GST recovered''). This is unlike DART,
whose \texttt{fnlttXbrl} package is numeric-only (\S\ref{sys:dart}). One current limit: until the standard
BSE label linkbase is wired (I4), a narrative's \texttt{section\_title} is the raw concept name rather than a
human label --- the prose \emph{bodies} are complete.

\section{Repositories}\label{sec:india-repos}

There is no single ``Indian EDGAR.'' A listed Indian company's financial statements are lodged, in the same
Ind-AS XBRL, with more than one repository, under the market regulator's disclosure rules (\textbf{SEBI}'s
Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements, Regulation~33 --- quarterly and annual financial results).
The three repositories differ sharply in \emph{access}, which is what decides where \texttt{EDGAR.jl}
fetches from (Table~\ref{tab:india-repos}).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{Indian financial-disclosure repositories and their accessibility.}
\label{tab:india-repos}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.16\textwidth}p{0.30\textwidth}p{0.24\textwidth}p{0.18\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Repository} & \textbf{What it is} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{NSE} & National Stock Exchange corporate-filings portal; the results XBRL of every NSE-listed company & \textbf{Keyless} JSON API (cookie-gated); direct Ind-AS \texttt{.xml} URLs & \textbf{primary channel} (planned) \\
\textbf{BSE} & Bombay Stock Exchange filings portal; \emph{publisher} of the \texttt{in-bse-fin} taxonomy & \textbf{Keyless} JSON API (endpoint not yet mapped) & second channel / cross-check (planned) \\
\textbf{MCA21} & Ministry of Corporate Affairs statutory registry; every company's annual AOC-4 XBRL & \textbf{Paid}, per-document; no bulk API & \emph{out of scope} (wrong door) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{NSE --- the primary channel (keyless)}
The National Stock Exchange exposes a corporate-filings \textbf{financial-results} endpoint that behaves
exactly like the discovery-to-XBRL seam of EDINET and DART, but with \textbf{no API key} --- only a browser
\emph{cookie bootstrap}. A query by ticker symbol returns a JSON array, one object per results filing, and
\textbf{each object carries a direct, keyless URL to the Ind-AS XBRL instance} (plus the ISIN, the
consolidated/standalone flag, the audited flag and the financial year). This is the repository the adapter
is built on. The access flow is three requests:

\begin{lstlisting}[style=term]
# 1. Bootstrap cookies from the homepage (a browser User-Agent is required).
% curl -c cookies.txt -A "$UA" https://www.nseindia.com/

# 2. Discover a company's results filings (keyless JSON; send the cookies + a Referer).
% curl -b cookies.txt -A "$UA" -H "Referer: https://www.nseindia.com/" \
    "https://www.nseindia.com/api/corporates-financial-results?index=equities&symbol=RELIANCE&period=Annual"

# 3. Fetch the Ind-AS XBRL instance named in each record's `xbrl` field.
% curl -b cookies.txt -A "$UA" -H "Referer: https://www.nseindia.com/" \
    https://nsearchives.nseindia.com/corporate/xbrl/INDAS_104634_1106038_22042024074704.xml
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent Each discovery record looks like the following (fields abridged); the \texttt{xbrl} URL is the
parse target, and \texttt{isin} is the identity key:

\begin{lstlisting}[style=term]
{
  "companyName":   "Reliance Industries Limited",
  "isin":          "INE002A01018",
  "consolidated":  "Consolidated",          # or "Standalone" -- filers lodge both
  "audited":       "Audited",
  "financialYear": "01-Apr-2023 To 31-Mar-2024",
  "period":        "Annual",
  "indAs":         "Ind-AS New",
  "xbrl":          "https://nsearchives.nseindia.com/corporate/xbrl/INDAS_104634_1106038_22042024074704.xml"
}
\end{lstlisting}

\noindent Two practical notes. First, the cookie bootstrap is mandatory --- the API returns nothing to a
cookieless client, and NSE runs bot detection, so a realistic \texttt{User-Agent} and the \texttt{Referer}
header matter; the session cookies expire and must be refreshed (refresh-on-failure, \emph{not} a retry
loop). Second, an NSE filing is a \textbf{single \texttt{.xml} instance} --- the linkbases are \emph{not}
bundled (the standard-taxonomy-only shape also seen at Companies House), so classification leans on the
presentation roles plus the canonical concept anchors rather than a bundled presentation linkbase.

\subsection{BSE --- the taxonomy publisher and second channel}
The Bombay Stock Exchange hosts the same Regulation~33 results filings and, importantly, is the
\textbf{publisher of the taxonomy} the instances are written in (the \texttt{in-bse-fin} namespace,
\S\ref{sec:india-taxonomy}). BSE also exposes a \textbf{keyless} JSON API (a probe returns HTTP~200), which
makes it the natural authoritative \emph{second} channel: because both exchanges publish the same company's
same filing, one can be cross-checked against the other as a same-document oracle. The exact
announcement/results endpoint and its six-digit \emph{scrip-code} addressing are \textbf{not yet mapped};
NSE alone is sufficient for the initial adapter, and BSE is deferred to the cross-check work.

\subsection{MCA21 --- the registrar (out of scope)}
MCA21 is the Ministry of Corporate Affairs' statutory registry, where \emph{every} incorporated company ---
listed or not --- files its annual return and financial statements (the AOC-4 XBRL form for the classes
required to tag). It is the most \emph{complete} repository, but access is \textbf{paid and per-document}
(document-purchase fees, no bulk or keyless API), which makes it the wrong door for XBRL extraction. The
listed-company financials that MCA21 holds are the same statements the exchanges publish for free, so
\texttt{EDGAR.jl} takes the exchange channel and treats MCA21 as out of scope.

\subsection{Taxonomy --- \texttt{in-bse-fin}, IFRS names under a national namespace}\label{sec:india-taxonomy}
A real NSE instance (Reliance, consolidated, FY ending 31~March~2024) resolves the taxonomy question that
governs classification. \textbf{Every} reporting concept sits in a \emph{single national namespace},
\texttt{in-bse-fin} (\texttt{http://www.bseindia.com/xbrl/fin/2020-03-31/in-bse-fin}, BSE's ``Financial
Results'' taxonomy) --- there are \textbf{zero} bare \texttt{ifrs-full} tags. But the concept
\emph{local names are IFRS-identical}: \texttt{in-bse-fin:Assets},
\texttt{in-bse-fin:AdjustmentsForDepreciationAndAmortisationExpense},
\texttt{in-bse-fin:AmountOfItemThatWillBeReclassifiedToProfitAndLoss} are the verbatim \texttt{ifrs-full}
element names, merely re-namespaced.

This is the \textbf{inverse of Korea}. K-IFRS filings tag under \emph{bare} \texttt{ifrs-full}, so DART
needed no vocabulary and no rewriting (\S\ref{sec:dart-kifrs}); India tags the \emph{same} IFRS concepts
under a national prefix. Because \texttt{EDGAR.jl} classifies by \textbf{namespace, not prefix}, an
\texttt{in-bse-fin} concept will not match the \texttt{ifrs-full} vocabulary as-is. \texttt{EDGAR.jl}
therefore classifies India through a \textbf{dedicated \texttt{vocab\_indas.jl}} keyed on the
\texttt{in-bse-fin} namespace --- the same by-namespace national-vocabulary pattern as Japan
(\texttt{vocab\_jpgaap}) and the UK (\texttt{vocab\_ukgaap}), rather than rewriting concepts onto
\texttt{ifrs-full}. Because the local names are IFRS-identical, that vocabulary is largely the IFRS concept
set re-keyed under \texttt{in-bse-fin}, plus the genuinely India-specific extensions. (This is phase~I3; the
offline parse and raw \texttt{facts} already work without it.)

\medskip\noindent \textbf{Two national namespaces, one pattern.} The Reliance example above is
\texttt{in-bse-fin} (the old \texttt{corporates-financial-results} feed). SEBI's current \textbf{Integrated
Filing} framework re-namespaces the \emph{same} IFRS-derived concepts once more, under \texttt{in-capmkt}
(\texttt{www.sebi.gov.in/xbrl/2025-01-31/in-capmkt}) --- so India has \emph{two} national taxonomy families,
not one. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} classifies each by its namespace: \texttt{vocab\_indas} for \texttt{in-bse-fin},
a parallel \texttt{vocab\_incapmkt} for \texttt{in-capmkt} (which also anchors the insurers' premium-based
revenue accounts). The per-sector templates of both families are Table~\ref{tab:india-formats}
(\S\ref{sec:india-formats}).

\section{Entities covered}\label{sec:india-entities}

Unlike EDINET or DART --- one regulator, one inclusion rule --- ``which companies can I reach?'' has a
\textbf{different answer per repository}, because the three channels cover different universes
(Table~\ref{tab:india-entities}). All three receive the \emph{same} Ind-AS results filing under SEBI
Reg.~33; they differ in \emph{which} entities are obliged to lodge with them. \texttt{EDGAR.jl} builds on the
exchange channels, so its covered set is \textbf{listed issuers}; the registrar's far larger all-companies
universe is out of scope (paid).

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{Indian repositories cover different entity universes (hence a per-repository inclusion rule).}
\label{tab:india-entities}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.13\textwidth}p{0.37\textwidth}p{0.18\textwidth}p{0.18\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Repository} & \textbf{Inclusion criterion} & \textbf{Universe (order of mag.)} & \textbf{In \texttt{EDGAR.jl}} \\
\midrule
\textbf{NSE} & equity listed on the National Stock Exchange (files Reg.~33 results) & $\sim$2{,}000 issuers & \textbf{primary} --- extracted \\
\textbf{BSE} & equity listed on the Bombay Stock Exchange --- a \emph{superset}: includes BSE-only SMEs and smaller issuers & $\sim$5{,}000+ issuers & second channel (planned) \\
\textbf{MCA21} & \emph{every} incorporated company --- listed or unlisted, public or private & $>$1 million active & \emph{out of scope} (paid) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{NSE-listed and BSE-listed issuers}
The exchange channels cover \textbf{listed equity issuers} filing quarterly and annual financial results
under SEBI Reg.~33. Most large companies are listed on \emph{both} exchanges (dual-listed), so NSE and BSE
overlap heavily at the top; BSE's roll is the larger and messier (many BSE-only SMEs and small issuers that
NSE does not carry). \texttt{EDGAR.jl} takes NSE as the primary channel and BSE as the authoritative second
channel and cross-check (\S\ref{sec:india-repos}); an entity is identified by its ISIN, with the NSE symbol
and BSE scrip code as secondary schemes. Each filing is lodged in both \textbf{standalone} and
\textbf{consolidated} form (the results API flags which); \texttt{EDGAR.jl} picks per request, defaulting to
consolidated.

\subsection{MCA21 --- all incorporated companies (out of scope)}
MCA21 additionally holds the annual accounts of \emph{every} registered company --- unlisted and private
included --- a universe orders of magnitude larger (India has over a million active companies). It is the
only channel that reaches \emph{unlisted} entities, but access is paid and per-document
(\S\ref{sec:india-repos}), so \texttt{EDGAR.jl} does not use it; the listed-company statements it holds are
the same ones the exchanges publish for free.

\paragraph{Boundary.} The results API surveyed here covers \textbf{equity issuers} (Reg.~33). Other
SEBI-regulated vehicles that also publish financial results --- REITs, InvITs, mutual funds --- file under
\emph{separate} regulations and endpoints, not yet surveyed; they are outside the current India scope.

\section{Forms and statement types}\label{sec:india-forms}

\textbf{SEBI} --- the Securities and Exchange Board of India --- is India's capital-markets regulator, the
analogue of the U.S.\ SEC (statutory under the SEBI Act, 1992). Listed companies disclose to the exchanges
(NSE, BSE) chiefly under the \textbf{SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations,
2015} (``LODR''), with the takeover code (SAST, 2011), the insider-trading rules (PIT, 2015) and the
capital-issue regulations (ICDR, 2018) governing the rest. Disclosure is keyed to these \emph{regulations},
not to a catalogue of numbered forms as at the SEC: a listed company files a whole \emph{universe} of
documents (Table~\ref{tab:india-forms}), of which \texttt{EDGAR.jl} extracts the one that is XBRL-tagged
financial data --- the periodic \textbf{financial results} --- and reaches the others for discovery as the
adapter (and the PDF-extraction track) matures. Each disclosure class is detailed in a subsection below; the
financial-statement structure of the periodic results --- the per-company-class \emph{format templates}
(\S\ref{sec:india-formats}), the taxonomies (\S\ref{sec:india-taxonomies}) and the statement types and their
classification (\S\ref{sec:india-statements}) --- follows at the end.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The Indian exchange-disclosure landscape --- each class detailed in a subsection below.}
\label{tab:india-forms}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.24\textwidth}p{0.38\textwidth}p{0.12\textwidth}p{0.11\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Class ($\to$ subsection)} & \textbf{Documents (regulation)} & \textbf{Format} & \textbf{In EDGAR.jl} \\
\midrule
Periodic reports & quarterly \& annual results (LODR 33); annual report (LODR 34) & XBRL / PDF & results extracted \\
Material-event disclosures & board outcomes, price-sensitive events (LODR 30); corporate actions & PDF / metadata & \emph{not yet} \\
Shareholding disclosures & quarterly shareholding pattern (LODR 31) & XBRL & \emph{not yet} \\
Ownership \& insider & insider trades (PIT~7), substantial acquisition (SAST) & metadata / PDF & \emph{not yet} \\
Tender offers \& mergers & open offers (SAST), schemes of arrangement & PDF & \emph{not yet} \\
Issuance disclosures & IPO / rights offer documents (ICDR), buy-backs & PDF & \emph{not yet} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{Periodic reports}\label{sec:india-periodic}
The scheduled financial filings. The \textbf{financial results} (LODR Regulation~33) --- filed
\textbf{quarterly} (Q1--Q3) and \textbf{annually} --- are India's analogue of the 10-Q/10-K and the
\emph{only} XBRL-tagged financial filing, the one \texttt{EDGAR.jl} extracts. They go to \emph{both} NSE and
BSE, each period in a \textbf{standalone} and a \textbf{consolidated} version (the API flags which;
\texttt{EDGAR.jl} defaults to consolidated). The annual filing --- the Q4 results carrying the full-year
column --- holds the audited annual statements; \emph{what} it contains depends on the company class (its
\emph{format template}, \S\ref{sec:india-formats}), and the statements are detailed in
\S\ref{sec:india-statements}. The fuller \textbf{annual report} (LODR Regulation~34) --- the complete
accounts with the notes and the statement of changes in equity that the condensed results omit --- is also a
periodic filing, but a PDF, and a candidate for the PDF-extraction track rather than the XBRL path.

\subsection{Material-event disclosures}\label{sec:india-material-events}
Event-driven, ad-hoc disclosures of material corporate developments (LODR Regulation~30) --- entirely
different in nature from the scheduled periodic reports, and the exact analogue of the SEC's Material-Event
Disclosures (Form 8-K, \S\ref{sec:edgar-material-events}). A company must promptly inform the exchanges of
deemed-material events (acquisitions, board-meeting outcomes, fund-raising, changes of directors / KMP /
auditors, credit-rating changes, insolvency proceedings, \ldots) and of other events its materiality policy
deems material, each as an \textbf{announcement with a PDF attachment} (the exchange exposes the feed keyless
--- confirmed live: each record carries a timestamp and an attachment URL). \textbf{Corporate actions} ---
dividends, bonus issues, stock splits and book-closure / ex-dates --- belong here too (a board-declared
dividend is itself a Regulation~30 event), and are additionally published as a structured keyless feed of
dates and ratios. All of this is PDF and metadata, not XBRL financial data --- \emph{not yet extracted}; the
announcement PDFs are candidates for the PDF-extraction track.

\subsection{Shareholding disclosures}\label{sec:india-shareholding}
The quarterly \textbf{shareholding pattern} (LODR Regulation~31) --- the promoter / public / institutional
ownership breakdown --- is itself filed as \textbf{XBRL}, on a template separate from the financial results.
\emph{Not yet handled}; a natural second XBRL target after the financial results.

\subsection{Ownership and insider disclosures}\label{sec:india-ownership}
Changes in \emph{control and insider} holdings, under two regimes. The insider-trading rules (PIT, 2015)
require promoters, directors and designated persons to disclose their trades --- the exchange's
insider-trading feed lists the acquirer names, quantities and dates (confirmed live). The takeover code
(SAST, 2011) requires disclosure of \textbf{substantial acquisitions} of shares or voting rights past a
threshold. Structured metadata / PDF; \emph{not yet handled}.

\subsection{Tender offers and mergers}\label{sec:india-tender}
Control transactions: \textbf{open offers} under the takeover code (SAST) --- the Indian tender offer,
triggered when an acquirer crosses the control threshold --- and \textbf{schemes of arrangement} (mergers,
demergers) sanctioned by the National Company Law Tribunal and filed with the exchange. Offer documents and
scheme papers are PDF; \emph{not yet handled}.

\subsection{Issuance disclosures}\label{sec:india-issuance}
Raising capital: the offer documents for \textbf{public and rights issues} under the capital-issue
regulations (ICDR, 2018) --- the draft red-herring prospectus and the prospectus --- and \textbf{buy-backs}.
These are among the richest PDF documents in the universe; \emph{not yet handled} (a PDF-track candidate).

\subsection{Result format templates by company class}\label{sec:india-formats}
Unlike a uniform IFRS regime (or K-IFRS at DART, \S\ref{sec:dart-forms}), \textbf{SEBI does not prescribe one
results format: it prescribes a different format per \emph{company class}}, and the repository serves each as
a \emph{distinct} XBRL template, identified by the instance's \textbf{filename prefix}
(Table~\ref{tab:india-formats}). A general company's \texttt{INDAS\_} template reports
\texttt{RevenueFromOperations}; a bank's \texttt{BANKING\_} template has \emph{no such concept}, reporting
\texttt{InterestEarned} / \texttt{InterestExpended}, \texttt{Deposits} and \texttt{Advances} (the Banking
Regulation Act shape); an NBFC files \texttt{NBFC\_INDAS\_}. The adapter must branch on the prefix --- a
general-format classifier or oracle silently misses a bank's income statement. India's forms are genuinely
its own; they do not map onto another jurisdiction's shapes.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{The SEBI results formats \texttt{EDGAR.jl} classifies, across the \emph{two} India taxonomy
families --- each a distinct XBRL template, keyed by the instance filename prefix. ``bundled'' = the
format's presentation, label \emph{and} calculation linkbases ship with \texttt{EDGAR.jl} for full offline
classification, labels and roll-up checks; ``verified'' = confirmed against a real filing.}
\label{tab:india-formats}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.26\textwidth}p{0.18\textwidth}p{0.28\textwidth}p{0.13\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Template (prefix)} & \textbf{Company class} & \textbf{Characteristic top-line concepts} & \textbf{Status} \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\textbf{\texttt{in-bse-fin}} --- the \texttt{corporates-financial-results} feed (\texttt{NSEFilings}), frozen $\sim$FY2024} \\
\midrule
\texttt{INDAS\_} & General (Ind-AS) & \texttt{RevenueFromOperations}; Ind-AS P\&L, BS, cash-flow, segment & verified; bundled \\
\texttt{BANKING\_} & Banks & \texttt{InterestEarned} / \texttt{InterestExpended}; \texttt{Deposits}, \texttt{Advances} & verified; bundled \\
\texttt{NBFC\_INDAS\_} & Non-banking financial companies & Ind-AS on the NBFC schedule & verified; bundled \\
\texttt{(schemaRef-routed)} & Other than Banks (legacy) & pre-Ind-AS results shape & bundled; unvalidated \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{4}{@{}l}{\textbf{\texttt{in-capmkt}} --- SEBI \textbf{Integrated Filing} (\texttt{IntegratedFilings}), the current framework, all sectors} \\
\midrule
\texttt{INTEGRATED\_FILING\_INDAS\_} & General (Ind-AS) & \texttt{RevenueFromOperations} & verified; bundled \\
\texttt{INTEGRATED\_FILING\_BANKING\_} & Banks & \texttt{InterestEarned}, \texttt{Deposits}, \texttt{Advances} & bundled \\
\texttt{INTEGRATED\_FILING\_NBFC\_INDAS\_} & NBFCs & Ind-AS on the NBFC schedule & bundled \\
\texttt{INTEGRATED\_FILING\_LI\_} & Life insurers & \texttt{GrossPremiumIncome}, \texttt{NetSurplusDeficit} & verified; bundled \\
\texttt{INTEGRATED\_FILING\_GI\_} & General insurers & \texttt{GrossPremiumsWritten}, \texttt{UnderwritingProfitOrLoss} & verified; bundled \\
